Wiring implemented and validated without scoring** (`src/nfl_ats/xlg06_prior_feature.py`, 6 leakage/contract tests, 2024-slice coverage ~1.00 at sane magnitudes). No registry entry (a fitted parameter is not an effect). (WP11/WP47) **2026-09-11 (XLG-06 lane, Stage 5): the prior was rebuilt end-to-end and scored on a fresh window; the row is now DONE for its stated scope.** The 2026-09-03 Stage 1-4 code (`src/nfl_ats/xlg06_prior.py`, `xlg06_crosswalk.py`, `xlg06_prior_feature.py` and their tests) was removed by the 2026-09-10 repository cut (b7ed31d, "60 src modules imported by nothing but their own tests"), and the Stage-4 family `xlg06_rookie_prior_on_production` could never be assigned because it was declared without `--acknowledge-mined` and every opener block intersects the 2018-2025 ledger, leaving it 0 eligible windows. This lane declared `xlg06_rookie_prior_surplus_on_production` (opener, `--acknowledge-mined`), was assigned **[2020, 2021]**, and rebuilt the whole chain self-contained in `scripts/xlg06_rookie_priors_screen.py` with the predeclaration frozen in `docs/rookie_priors_on_production.md` before any ATS outcome was scored. **Prior**: per-rookie, deadline-visible, walk-forward ridge on log(overall draft pick), class-standardised pre-draft consensus grade and final-college-season CFB usage share, fit only on strictly earlier draft classes, mapped onto the injury-value NFL scale (skill_epa/100 offensive snaps for RB/WR/TE, defense_disruption/100 defensive snaps for DL/LB/DB) and reported as a standardised surplus over the position-group generic replacement, with the training-residual RMSE as its explicit uncertainty; QB and OL have no target on that scale at all and get a pooled slot-and-grade channel labelled `slot_only`. Decay frozen at Stage 3's N0=300 snaps, weight(n)=N0/(n+N0), not retuned. **Identity chain**: 3,077 drafted 2014-2025, 3,066 linked on (draft year, overall pick), 3,062 passing the surname-or-ESPN-id guard (99.5%), 99.61% surname agreement; CFB usage present for 753 of 827 (91.1%) offence-arm rookies. **Coverage**: 100% of rookie starters (300+ snaps) carry a prior in every class 2015-2025, 69-117 per season. **Prior vs realised first-season NFL value**, draft-class-blocked, 20k resamples: Pearson **+0.1304, 95% [+0.0934, +0.1713], P+ 1.000** on n=1,288 (Spearman +0.1675); split-half by draft year replicates on both halves (odd +0.1488 [+0.0917,+0.2036]; even +0.1148 [+0.0726,+0.1656], both P+ 1.000); by arm, defence **+0.1662 [+0.1093,+0.2271] P+ 1.000** carries it and offence +0.0336 [-0.0848,+0.1641] P+ 0.715 leans the same way. Outcome split-half reliability (odd vs even weeks of the rookie season) r=0.2596, Spearman-Brown 0.4122 -- non-zero, so `no_split_half_reliability` is inadmissible anywhere in this lane. **Market read** (opener, 2020-2025, 1,611 team-games with a rookie QB/OL/pass-rusher starting under a frozen pregame definition): weeks 1-6 cover **48.39%** (n=498, excess over 50% 95% [-5.02,+1.77] pts, P+ 0.173), weeks 7+ **51.03%** (n=1,113, [-0.67,+2.72], P+ 0.881), early-minus-late **-2.64 pts [-6.43,+1.11], P+ 0.085** (so the probability early is the worse half is 0.915). Rookie OL carries it (46.94% early vs 51.30% late on 1,206 team-games). **On-production screen**, tilt TOWARD the side whose decayed snap-share-weighted rookie prior surplus is larger, stacked on production `weak_stack`, opener grade, window [2020,2021]: 456 games/35 weeks, 234 flagged, **118 forced picks changed**, candidate **55.70%** vs baseline 53.29% = **+2.412 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [-2.450, +7.456], P+ 0.832** (season-blocked [+0.424,+4.545] P+ 1.000 on 2 blocks only, not treated as informative). Positive control +46.711 pts P+ 1.000 both blockings -- harness proven sensitive. Secondary full 2020-2025 opener archive (not a separate spend): **+1.065 pts [-1.378,+3.508], P+ 0.811** on 1,503 games/107 weeks, 325 picks changed, 55.62% vs 54.56%; per season 2020 +4.545 (P+ 0.878), 2021 +0.424 (0.558), 2022 +0.806 (0.588), 2023 -3.008 (0.100), 2024 +4.511 (0.993), 2025 -0.375 (0.428). All 16 measurements recorded via `nfl-ats weak-signals record` (registry 6,046 -> 6,063, 16 rows) and the window spent via `nfl-ats rotation record` with verdict `unresolved`; no interval is a resolved wrong sign and no positive control bounds anything, so every row is `unresolved_below_power`. **Activation-visibility control (the number to actually use)**: a rookie appears in `snap_counts` for week w only if he was ACTIVE, which a Tuesday deadline does not know, and the frozen feature zeroes a rookie's contribution in any week he does not appear. `scripts/xlg06_rookie_priors_screen.py leakage-control` removes that bit -- once a rookie has appeared, his last-known trailing share and snap total carry forward to every later week regardless -- and rescores both arms on the same full 2020-2025 archive (not a window draw): observed-presence +1.065 pts P+ 0.811 vs **deadline-clean +0.466 pts, 95% [-2.076, +3.102], P+ 0.648**, 329 picks changed, 91.4% pick agreement. Removing the peek costs about 56% of the effect; the window read carries the same peek. Recorded `xlg06_rookie_prior_surplus_tilt_activation_clean`. **Decision, stated before the caveats**: +0.466 pts at P+ 0.648 is a 65/35 lean on a forced-pick pool, so on expected value it belongs on the played card pending the composition check that an overlay positive alone can still be negative stacked. **Two further disclosed weaknesses**: (1) the frozen 80th-percentile firing threshold is dragged down by prior seasons that carry no priors at all, so the rule fires on 42.4% of full-schedule games and changes 26% of in-window picks instead of the intended ~20% -- frozen before scoring and deliberately NOT retuned on the window, a successor predeclaration should compute the percentile only over seasons that have priors; (2) the window intersects the 2018-2025 multiplicity ledger (RWB-16), acknowledged at declaration, discount accordingly. **Separate live defect found while building this, for someone to pick up**: `weekly_rosters.pfr_id` -- the column `src/nfl_ats/players.py::_stable_crosswalk` maps snap counts through -- is 0.29% populated for `position == "OL"` (0.00% for T, 0.74% for G, 0.25% for C) against 60-75% elsewhere, and covers only 3,738 of the 7,095 distinct `pfr_player_id` in `snap_counts.parquet` while `players.parquet` covers 7,066. Every production feature derived from snap counts through that crosswalk is silently dropping nearly every offensive lineman; this lane worked around it locally and did not touch `src/`. (XLG-06 lane) **2026-09-11 follow-up: wired as prospective challenger** `rookie_prior_surplus_tilt_overlay` (`ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE`, `artifacts/prospective/challengers.json`, display name "Rookie-starter value tilt" in `CHALLENGER_DISPLAY_NAMES`), a no-window-cost post-prediction tilt mirroring `post_bye_new_playcaller_back_overlay`/`veteran_rest_back_overlay`'s structure (`src/nfl_ats/rookie_prior_surplus_tilt_overlay.py`). It reuses the frozen construction directly from `scripts/xlg06_rookie_priors_screen.py` -- `fit_priors`/`build_prior_inputs`, `N0_SNAPS`=300 decay, the 80th-percentile walk-forward firing threshold, `carry_forward_presence` and `week1_expected_share` for the last-known-share-carried-forward rule -- in the ACTIVATION-CLEAN form only, and exercises the same OL snap-count crosswalk workaround via `screen.load_snap_panel()`'s `_pfr_to_gsis` (weekly_rosters linked first, players.parquet backfilled) so linemen who have actually snapped are not silently dropped. Because the screen's own `rookie_week_state`/`team_week_surplus`/`game_level_feature` helpers depend on a cached, gitignored local artifact and an inner join against `snap_counts.parquet` that is empty for any season with zero completed games (measured 2026-09-11: the newest local snapshot carries zero season-2026 rows, true for every rookie in his own team's Week 1), the overlay refits the prior fresh every run and adds a live-only branch: a current-draft-class rookie absent from this season's snap panel is assigned to his team via `players.parquet`'s `latest_team` field, with his Week 1 contribution from the same frozen `week1_expected_share` walk-forward fallback. Confirmed live against the active 2026 Week 1 card (`artifacts/margin_predictions/2026-week-01-20260910T210852Z`, 16 games, threshold 0.132863 computed from 2015-2025 history only): 11 of 16 games flagged, 6 forced-pick flips recorded to the challenger ledger without touching the served card (`2026_01_ATL_PIT`->PIT, `2026_01_CLE_JAX`->CLE, `2026_01_DAL_NYG`->NYG, `2026_01_MIA_LV`->LV, `2026_01_SF_LA`->LA, `2026_01_TB_CIN`->TB; the other 5 flagged games already agreed with production's raw pick). Registered evidence cites the activation-clean read (+0.466 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [-2.076, +3.102], P+ 0.648) as the number this challenger's own 2026+ ledger should track, disclosing the peeking window's +2.412/P+0.832 separately per AGENTS.md's provenance rule. |
| XLG-07 | ✅ | Cross-league availability semantics | Completed 2026-09-02 as a measured fail-closed feasibility answer (`docs/xlg07_availability_semantics.md`). Recent conference mandates prove pregame reporting exists prospectively, but the retained history is not admissible for learning availability: six Big Ten 2023 PDFs cover only six weeks, expose no per-team issue timestamp, and 53 of 71 unambiguously schedule-matched team pages sit in aggregate files created after kickoff; the SEC pilot recovered 137 rows from only three 2024 weeks/14 team-games, with just two contemporaneous Wayback states and a gated 2025 archive. Legacy all-FBS injury assets remain absent and roster status fields remain quarantined. Do not join to XLG-03, infer healthy players from absent rows, run an ATS experiment, or record a signal verdict; immutable prospective per-report collection is a separate future dataset. |
| XLG-08 | ✅ | FluView illness, CFB replication | **Done 2026-09-01 (WP7).** The NFL FluView home-market illness construct replicated on college football at zero NFL-window cost, on top of the frozen XLG-03 benchmark arm (`docs/fluview_cfb_replication.md`). Two prerequisites built: venue state from cfbfastR-data `team_info` at a pinned commit joined on CFBD/ESPN `team_id` (0 unresolved rows, no CFBD credit); the 19 CFB-only FluView states ingested into a separate directory. On 5,671 clean-core games (2017-2019, 2021-2025, close-proxy grade): home-market **-0.388 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [-1.272, +0.460], P+ 0.200**; away-market -0.423, [-1.553, +0.694], P+ 0.213; positive control +48.6 pts P+ 1.000. The NFL close-graded on-production direction (+0.969, P+ 0.792) did **not** replicate, in either CFB era; the CFB state-week trait's own split-half reliability replicates (Spearman-Brown 0.9856), so the mechanism is not refuted as noise. Both cells `unresolved_below_power` (`fluview_cfb_replication`, `--league cfb`); no rotation window spent. Method note: the benchmark's `fit_cfb_residual_model(feature_columns=...)` extension point makes "benchmark arm plus one column" a ~3-minute run. |

## Phase 3 — better football state

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| PBP-01 | ✅ | Versioned play-by-play ingestion | Partitioned Parquet snapshots and required-field contracts |
| PBP-02 | ✅ | Situation filters | Remove kneels and garbage time with versioned definitions |
| PBP-03 | ✅ | Drive table | Possessions, starting field position, points, success, and duration completed; ATS screen did not support promotion |
| PBP-04 | ✅ | Stable team efficiency | Early-down EPA, success, explosive rate, pressure, PROE |
| PBP-05 | ✅ | Opponent adjustment | Weekly time-decayed ridge offense/defense decomposition completed; no stable improvement, retained research-only. **One untested framing remains (2026-08-17):** every prior test *added* the adjusted columns on top of the raw ones, taking the design from 58 to 106–142 columns on ~4,700 rows at alpha 10, so signal was confounded with dimension inflation. The dimension-neutral **substitution** — same column count, adjusted defensive rates swapped in for raw ones — has never been run. Motivation is measured: split-half reliability of team per-play rates over 2009–2012 is **0.80 offense vs 0.46 defense** (full-season, Spearman-Brown; independently reproduced), so the active model's defensive per-play columns are roughly half noise, which is exactly what opponent adjustment exists to fix. **Screened on CFB 2026-08-17 and CLOSED** (`docs/cfb_opponent_adjustment.md`): the dimension-neutral swap moved clean-core margin MAE by -0.0003 points, `probability_positive` 0.463 on 9,093 games; isolating the opponent block from a time-weighting change bundled with it recovers +0.0005 MAE. A deliberate-leak positive control (adjustment fit on all of 2006-2025, so the columns see the future) moved MAE only **+0.0129** (P+ 0.984) -- so the null is measured rather than underpowered, and that figure is a **ceiling on the whole family**. The reliability argument is sound but does not translate: against a market-residual target the spread already prices team quality, so denoising those columns has almost nothing left to improve. A revisit needs a different target, not a better adjustment. See `docs/play_level_audit.md` |
| PBP-06 | ✅ | Special teams | Kicking, punting, returns, field position above expectation. **Built and screened 2026-08-19** (`docs/special_teams_battery.md`): fresh nflverse PBP fetched season-by-season and aggregated immediately to team-game/team-season tables (never persisted raw, referee-battery precedent). Reliability audit first, per the PBP-08 precedent: year-over-year Pearson r `punt_net_yards` +0.313 [+0.233,+0.391] (the strongest, comparable to PER-07's own +0.320 bar), `kickoff_return_yards` +0.158 [+0.073,+0.243], `punt_return_yards` +0.109 [+0.019,+0.196], `fg_oe` +0.065 [-0.022,+0.153] (weakest kept), `block_rate` -0.024 [-0.105,+0.060] (measured, scoped out of cells -- indistinguishable from noise even though it technically clears the admissible exclusion bar). 8 predeclared cells (4 traits x top/bottom quartile, team-perspective `team_covered`, week-blocked primary / season-blocked secondary bootstrap, 20,000 samples seed 20260819) all recorded `unresolved_below_power` -- none wrong-sign-resolved, none excludes zero, per AGENTS.md's binding rule. Two flagged leads (`probability_positive` outside [0.15, 0.85]): `special_teams_return_top_quartile` +0.499pts, week-blocked 95% [-0.074,+1.080], P+=0.955; `special_teams_punt_net_top_quartile` -0.389pts, week-blocked 95% [-0.967,+0.204], P+=0.095 (opposite the predeclared sign -- still unresolved, not refuted, since the interval does not sit entirely below zero). All 8 recorded to `registry/weak_signals.json` (`special_teams_*`, registry now 193 signals). Research-only; nothing wired into production. **CFB replication ran 2026-08-19** (`docs/cfb_special_teams_replication.md`, results recorded to `registry/weak_signals.json`): neither NFL lead replicates cross-league on the XLG-03 clean core (8,933 games, 17,866 team-rows). `cfb_special_teams_return_top_quartile` reads -0.337 pts, week-blocked 95% [-0.691, +0.021], P+ 0.032 — the opposite sign from the NFL cell it mirrors (+0.499 pts, P+ 0.955). `cfb_special_teams_punt_net_top_quartile` reads +0.307 pts, week-blocked 95% [-0.164, +0.791], P+ 0.897 — also a sign flip, but the other direction: CFB leans toward the originally predeclared positive sign that the NFL read itself inverted (NFL -0.389 pts, P+ 0.095). Both CFB entries `unresolved_below_power` (neither interval sits entirely on one side of zero); no challenger wiring from either league's special-teams read |
| PBP-07 | ✅ | Penalty state | **Not built, and NOT closed -- the market check is below detection power.** Team penalty rate is 0.0750 +/- 0.0101 with year-over-year reliability +0.261, so there is a real if modest trait to forecast. The market check (most-penalized quartile covers 49.85% vs least-penalized 50.52%) is a 0.67-point gap on quartile-sized samples and cannot separate "priced" from "too small to see" -- category 3 in `docs/pool_edge_plan.md`, not a negative. Treat as a weak-signal stacker input at most, never a standalone candidate and never worth a window. Separately, the construct this row actually names is not measurable from local data -- neither play-by-play snapshot carries `penalty_type` or a description column (45 columns; only `penalty` and `penalty_yards`), so "pre-snap discipline" would need a wider nflverse column pull. ~~Since total penalty discipline is already null against the market, that pull is not worth making~~ **Correction, 2026-08-20: the pull was made after all and it was cheap** (`docs/penalty_crew_tendencies.md`, build-next rank 1 in `docs/archive/data_source_scout_v4.md`) -- nflverse's upstream PBP schema does carry `penalty_type`/`penalty_team`, this project's own trimmed local snapshot was just dropping them. Widening extended `docs/referee_battery.md`'s already-reliable crew penalty-RATE battery (`mean_total` +0.370, 158 referee-season pairs) to penalty-TYPE-specific rates: Offensive Holding (+0.3226) and Defensive Holding (+0.2702) show real, moderate year-over-year persistence; Defensive Pass Interference does not (-0.0663, near zero). Four predeclared cells, all `unresolved_below_power`: the standout is a run-heavy home team facing a top-quartile Offensive-Holding-calling crew covering LESS, +0.1390 pts, P+ 0.9015, consistent direction across both eras (2016-2020 P+ 0.895, 2021-2025 P+ 0.662, real magnitude change not a sign flip); a heavy home underdog facing a top-quartile flag-heavy crew at the OPENER grade leans +0.1056 pts, P+ 0.9204, stable across both opener-era halves despite n_flag=9/era. A DPI-tilt/pass-heavy-favorite cell and a flag-rate/high-total cell both stay near coin-flip or era-inconsistent. Not a standalone candidate; a future pooled read alongside the existing referee-battery cells (same `accuracy_points` unit) is the natural next step, not a promotion decision **2026-09-01 (WP22):** a live, point-in-time capture of the UPCOMING week's officiating assignment now exists (`src/nfl_ats/referee_assignments_capture.py`, scheduler job `referee_assignments_wed` Wed 15:00 ET, `docs/referee_assignments_capture.md`), fetching Football Zebras' weekly assignments post (the only public source; `operations.nfl.com` has no weekly page, verified) and joining the referee's name to `officials.parquet`'s crew traits (17/17 after one alias, "Ron Torbert" -> "Ronald Torbert"; validated live on 2025 Week 18, 16/16 games). Publish timing measured across 10 sampled 2025 weeks is never before Tuesday afternoon (latest normal-week sample Wed 12:42 ET), so this capture structurally cannot feed the Tuesday-lock/opener card -- it feeds a late-week refresh, which is exactly what `crew_tilt_refresh_v1` (WP47, in flight) wires for the two P+ ~0.90 cells. This satisfies the point-in-time precondition PBP-10 names; neither cell has been re-scored prospectively yet. (WP22, 2026-09-01) **2026-09-02 implementation reconciliation:** the dedicated immutable widened aggregate (`scripts/fetch_penalty_type_snapshot.py`; `game_penalty_types.parquet`) has required-field and count-alignment contracts; prior-season team/crew rate and penalty-type traits have same-game leakage regressions; the scheduled immutable referee-assignment capture is joined and fail-closed; and registered `crew_tilt_refresh_v1` is wired to `refresh-picks --record-decisions` as an opt-in, append-only, late-week challenger that cannot alter the played card. The historical cells remain `unresolved_below_power`; prospective settlement is evidence follow-through, not unfinished PBP-07 implementation. (2026-09-02) |
| PBP-08 | ✅ | Scheme/matchup interactions | **Completed and reconciled 2026-09-02** (`docs/pbp08_matchup_screen.md`, `docs/team_style.md`). Pregame-safe prior-four-game protection/pressure and explosive-pass/coverage interactions were built and screened alongside reliable formation/pace personality traits; every category-3 cell is preserved in `registry/weak_signals.json`. The strongest playable descendants are dual-tracked rather than silently promoted (`pbp08_protection_mismatch_tilt_overlay`, `pace_mismatch_dog_tilt_overlay`, and `team_style_pace_production_feature`), with future-game flag construction, leakage boundaries, no-op behavior, and append-only recording pinned by 31 focused tests on 2026-09-02. Completion means the named interaction families are implemented and evaluated; their unresolved measurements remain live evidence, not settled negatives. |
| PBP-09 | ✅ | Pace and possession forecast | Closed on measurement, not built (2026-08-17; `docs/play_level_audit.md`). Game play volume has almost nothing to forecast and no link to the outcome we care about: on 2009–2012 (1,024 games, no window spent) plays/game is 125.5 with sd 8.7, a coefficient of variation of **0.070**; forecasting it from both teams' prior pace reaches only **R² 0.041**, shaving ~2% off the residual sd. Worse for the premise, `corr(plays, |margin|) = −0.20` — blowouts have *fewer* plays, which is clock-killing and kneel-downs reading backwards from the result — and `corr(drives, |margin|) = +0.004`, i.e. zero. Independently reproduced by a second measurement before this row was changed. A pregame pace forecast has no path to ATS value; reopen only if some mechanism other than volume is proposed |
| PBP-10 | ✅ | Referee effects | **Implemented and reconciled 2026-09-02.** Point-in-time weekly assignments are captured immutably before each game's decision deadline (`src/nfl_ats/referee_assignments_capture.py`; `docs/referee_assignments_capture.md`), and the playable `crew_tilt_refresh_v1` challenger uses only each referee's prior completed season with frozen historical quartile cutpoints. The underlying crew traits survive the predeclared persistence screen (`mean_total` year-over-year r +0.3702; Offensive Holding +0.3226, 158 referee-season pairs each), while the stacked opener-grade comparison remains correctly recorded as `unresolved_below_power`: +0.3327 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [-0.1350,+0.8525], `probability_positive` 0.8825 (`registry/weak_signals.json`). The late-week path is opt-in and append-only and never mutates the played card. Focused point-in-time, leakage, forward-quartile, and recording contracts: 43 passed on 2026-09-02. |

## Phase 4 — players, coaches, injuries, and offseason priors

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| PER-01 | ✅ | Weekly roster/participation snapshots | Immutable weekly roster plus season-partitioned 2016–2025 participation snapshots, hashes, source regimes, and outcome-time contracts |
| PER-02 | ✅ | Quarterback state | **Implementation complete 2026-09-02** (`docs/quarterback_state_features.md`): the deterministic pregame builder now joins timestamped QB1/QB2 depth identity to the existing fixed or season-lagged starter probability and each named player's strictly prior EPA/dropback, CPOE, and experience state. Expected value mixes named QB1/QB2 states, exposes backup-minus-starter adjustments, and fails closed on uncovered injury seasons, stale depth, or missing history. Leakage regressions mutate current-game PBP plus post-decision depth/injury revisions. The fully namespaced, non-overlapping `quarterback_depth` family shares the availability resolver with `player_qb` but cannot overwrite its different prior-appearance identity semantics; it is registered but absent from every model profile. No ATS experiment, registry, or active-model change was made. |
| PER-04 | ✅ | Depth chart history | **Completed 2026-09-02** (`docs/historical_depth_charts.md`): official nflverse 2009–2024 weekly depth rows are archived immutably with the absent source observation timestamp kept null and a conservative effective timestamp one microsecond after that NFL week's final kickoff, so week-W identity can affect only later games. The PER-02 selector reuses this effective-time primitive for both legacy and true timestamped revisions. Measured snapshot `20260902T225115Z`: 22,962 QB rows, all 32 teams/all 16 requested seasons, named QB2 coverage 8,065/8,690 historical team-games (92.81%); all 510 Week-1 team-games deliberately fail closed, and 19 duplicated source identities are collapsed to one best rank with explicit conflict provenance. Snapshot/source hashes, coverage, chronology, stale/gap guards, and leakage tests are recorded. No ATS experiment, registry, feature profile, or active-model change. |
| PER-05 | ✅ | Snap-weighted player value | **Implementation complete; evidence remains appropriately unresolved.** `src/nfl_ats/players.py` builds the two registered `player_values` features from strictly prior snap-weighted offensive-skill EPA and defensive-disruption state, reliability shrinkage, lagged role share, and decision-time injury availability; `tests/test_players.py` pins current-game leakage, prior-only value updates, shrinkage, and learned-availability replacement. The separate season-lagged participation ratings and their target-season leakage canary are implemented in `src/nfl_ats/participation.py` / `tests/test_participation.py`. The box-score value profile reached 52.14%; the fixed participation extension reached 51.71%, but that negative/unresolved model evidence does not make the feature infrastructure unfinished. Stronger hierarchical value targets remain follow-on research under PER-09, not an unimplemented part of this row. **2026-09-11 defect found and fixed (`docs/ol_crosswalk_defect.md`; this row rather than PER-03 because it owns `src/nfl_ats/players.py` and the snap-derived features, while PER-03 owns the injury-report ingest the crosswalk joins against).** XLG-06 reported that `weekly_rosters.pfr_id` is empty for offensive linemen and inferred that every snap-derived production feature was dropping nearly all of them. Measured on the served snapshot `20260910T205112Z`: the first half reproduces — `_stable_crosswalk` resolved an id for **0.41%** of offensive-line snap rows vs 75.5% front / 86.5% skill — but the second half does not, because `attach_snap_player_ids`' name fallbacks carried final coverage to 98.84% for the OL. The real residue was concentrated, not diffuse: PFR's snap sheet writes "Orlando Brown Jr." where nflverse's roster writes "Orlando Brown", so the name fallback missed **every-down starting tackles with a suffix** — 21 players / 716 snap rows over 2019–2025, including Jedrick Wills Jr. (CLE 2022, 1,154 offensive snaps), Orlando Brown Jr. (KC 2021–22, CIN 2023–25), Jon Runyan Jr. (GB/NYG 2020–25), Chris Harris Jr. (LAC 2020–21, 749 defensive snaps) — each carrying a role share of zero, so their injury-report appearances moved `injury_offensive_line_unavailability` and the returning-snap-share family not at all. One case was a misattribution rather than a miss: PFR `WillJo10` is Jonah Williams the CIN/ARI left tackle (`00-0035629`), and the name fallback credited 74 rows of his snaps to the Rams/Saints defensive lineman of the same name (`00-0035944`). **Fix:** `_stable_crosswalk(rosters, players=None)` now resolves through the `players.parquet` identity master first and the weekly rosters second, with an identity link accepted only when its `gsis_id` also appears in the roster universe (measured: without that guard six rows regress to ids like `BAT138483` that join to nothing). Coverage by position group goes 98.84→99.99 (OL), 99.59→99.98 (front), 99.39→100.00 (secondary), 99.80→99.97 (skill); 1,571 rows newly linked, 80 corrected, 0 lost; the identity table is a static person-to-person map and adds no dated information. **Consequence, graded chronologically:** both arms rebuilt from identical pinned snapshots (the disabled-crosswalk arm reproduces the served feature table exactly, 0 of 273 numeric columns differ), then scored with the production opener evaluator under active model `d49194e04945a5e5`. 42 columns move (all seven injury-unavailability metrics, both injury-value-lost metrics, the three returning-snap-share metrics, QB start probability via the refitted availability rates; lineup continuity does not move, since it keys on `player_key` with a `pfr:` fallback). At the opener 2020–2025, 1,503 graded games: **13 picks changed (0.86%)**, accuracy 54.558% → 54.491%, **−0.0665 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [−0.534, +0.400], `probability_positive` 0.387**; Brier −0.000034, 95% [−0.000124, +0.000054], `probability_positive` 0.226; per season −0.45 / +0.85 / +0.40 / 0.00 / 0.00 / −1.12. Every one of the 13 flips had a baseline probability inside [0.4968, 0.5052] and they split 7–6, so the grade is 13 coin flips and carries almost no information about the fix. Both rows recorded `unresolved_below_power` (family `ol_crosswalk_fix`); no closing ground applies — the per-season sign is not resolved and no positive control bounds a crosswalk-sized effect. Recommendation in the doc is to serve it on correctness with the adverse grade stated plainly; **this lane did not switch the served feature table or the active model**, which stays a deliberate separate act. |
| PER-06 | ✅ | Roster continuity | **Implementation complete 2026-09-02** (`docs/roster_continuity.md`): prior-game lineup overlap and one-week-delayed active-roster continuity now sit beside an isolated returning-snap offseason family. For target season T, the new family weights the latest safely observable current-season active roster by each named player's full T-1 offense, defense, and special-teams snaps; target/future snaps are structurally keyed only to later seasons. Undated weekly rosters remain delayed, so all Week-1 rows and the two 2017 Week-2 Hurricane Irma postponement rows fail closed. Measured on the immutable 20260817T184901Z player snapshot and 2014–2025 REG schedule: 5,918/5,920 (99.97%) Week-2+ team-games have all three values. The family is registered but absent from every existing profile; no ATS experiment, registry, or active-model decision changed. |
| PER-08 | ✅ | Transaction-aware preseason prior | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/preseason_prior.py`, `docs/transaction_aware_preseason_prior.md`): deterministic decision-time priors combine caller-supplied, common-unit QB, roster, coaching, draft, and free-agency adjustments with neutral default weights, optional half-life decay, source uncertainty, canonical teams, and explicit override priority. Both observed/effective timestamps must precede the cutoff; source-level audits retain selection reason, age, decay, weights, deltas, and uncertainty. A leakage regression proves future additions/mutations cannot change the prior. No outcome-fit weights, ATS screen, or active-model wiring was introduced. |
| PER-14 | ✅ | OL/skill prior features | Follow-up to the unit-APM gates (`docs/unit_apm_ratings.md`: OFF_SKILL SB 0.394, OFF_OL 0.325, both healthy): season-lagged unit ratings as game features. Builder unbuilt; ATS evaluation needs an open window. **2026-09-05 CX19:** Measured (`artifacts/experiments/unit_prior_features/inventory.json`): 4 summary files but 0 annual rating rows/seasons; exact-S-1 availability-checked join and leakage tests built, screen/continuity controls predeclared in `docs/unit_prior_features.md`, ATS unmeasured under the missing-artifact fallback (no fabricated registry effect). **2026-09-05 CX20:** Measured (artifacts/experiments/unit_prior_features/cx20_20260905/results.json): 1,280 annual team-unit rows for 2016-2025 produced; PER-14 opener screen recorded unresolved_below_power at -0.399202 accuracy points [-1.490591, +0.720862], probability_positive=0.2208 on 1,503 games, same-season oracle +0.133067 points, probability_positive=0.5503; dated continuity unknown. |
| PER-11 | ✅ | Learned play probability | Prior-season report/practice/position rates improved player Brier 0.09500 → 0.09056 and ATS 52.14% → 52.24%; blocked ATS intervals remain unresolved |
| PER-12 | ✅ | Expected role delivery | Closed at the intermediate target August 2026: the clipped two-part delivery model lost to both parents on delivered-share MAE in all 11 seasons (0.1888 vs 0.1606/0.1621) because injury-listed players who play at all deliver ~their full prior role (median ratio 1.01); no ATS rows were generated |
| PER-13 | ✅ | Reliability trait priors | Per-player durability from 16-season injury/participation history and roster-status volatility (including league suspensions from weekly roster status codes) as priors inside the learned-availability model; validate on the player-level availability target before any ATS screen **Stage 1 answered on the intermediate target (WP13, 2026-09-01; `docs/per13_durability_prior.md`, frozen before any Brier).** Six per-player durability columns (EB-shrunken residual-vs-designation rate, played-despite-listed rate, raw unavailability offset, history depth, on-roster-no-snap rate, reserve/suspension rate back to 2009; no hand-picked shrinkage -- beta-binomial and DerSimonian-Laird moment estimators per fold) added to a re-fit learned-availability baseline on PER-11's out-of-season protocol. On 52,382 player-games 2015-2024: Brier 0.09087 -> 0.08332, **+0.0075514**, week-blocked 95% [+0.0067549, +0.0083081], P+ 1.000; 10/10 seasons and 5/5 position groups favour it; positive control passed first; a post-hoc placebo (each player handed another's history) destroys the effect (-0.0000871), so this is row-level player information. Prior-seasons-only retains 51% of the gain. Recorded `per13_durability_prior_availability_brier` (units `brier`, reliability 0.793, `unresolved_below_power`). **The frozen EV gate is met, so Stage 2 -- an ATS test on top of PRODUCTION on a rotation-assigned window -- is warranted (queued as WP26).** Sober framing: PER-11's 0.00444 Brier gain bought only +0.10 ATS points, so this is not an ATS edge and is never pooled with `accuracy_points`. (WP13, 2026-09-01) |

## Phase 5 — weather, venue, rest, and travel

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| ENV-01 | ✅ | Forecast-time weather | Archive the forecast that existed at the decision timestamp. **2026-08-19: actual-weather mechanism screens ran first** — 8 predeclared cells from schedules' game-time actuals (`scripts/nfl_weather_battery_screen.py`, seasons 2009-2025, all recorded `unresolved_below_power`): wind/cold cells lean positive (high_wind_outdoor +0.16 pts P+ 0.757; warm_team_cold_late +0.16 P+ 0.972; dome_team_outdoors_cold +0.11 P+ 0.825) but are game-time actuals, hence UPPER BOUNDS on any pregame forecast feature. **Sourcing question answered 2026-08-19 evening (`docs/weather_forecast_sourcing.md`), by measurement not just documentation-reading**: Open-Meteo's Historical Forecast API is confirmed paid-only (free tier excludes it outright); NOAA's NDFD archive IS free and genuinely point-in-time — verified by downloading one real archived bulletin (`wmo/maxt/2022/12/11/YGRZ98_KWBN_202212112153`, WMO-header-stamped issuance 21:53 UTC, valid GRIB2 edition-2 payload, 4 embedded messages). Two free tiers: (1) AWS Open Data (`s3://noaa-ndfd-pds`), anonymous HTTPS, instant, but **measured** to cover 2020-present only (year prefixes checked live); (2) NCEI HAS/AIRS "NDFD - By WMO Header," **read** as free and covering 2004-present (the full project window) but request/order-gated, turnaround unverified. Needed elements (`maxt`, `temp`, `wspd`, `wgust`, `wdir`) all confirmed present. Real remaining cost is downstream, not access: no GRIB2 decoder (`pygrib`/`cfgrib`/`eccodes`) is installed in this repo's venv (measured), and no stadium lat/lon table exists yet (measured) — both are prerequisites before any point value can be extracted; HAS/AIRS turnaround for 2009-2019 is the next thing to actually test. Data-quality note: schedules' outdoor wind/temp is missing for 49% of 2022 and 22% of 2023 games. **2026-08-19: the archive was built, and the payoff screen ran** (`docs/forecast_archive_build.md`, `docs/forecast_weather_screen.md`). No GRIB2 decoder was needed after all — the build pivoted to the Iowa Environmental Mesonet's GFS-MOS JSON API (point-in-time station bulletins, no gridded decode). The pool-relevant `tuesday_noon` cutoff archive covers the full 2020-2025 REG population (1,615 games, 100% of 1,598 domestic games fetched OK): temp r=0.897 MAE=7.63°F bias=-5.80°F, wind r=0.387 MAE=4.89mph bias=+2.79mph vs. actuals. A new `kickoff_nearest` cutoff (added this session; ~~not pool-playable since the pool locks Tuesday noon~~ **owner-corrected 2026-08-20: this IS pool-playable, and is in fact the primary target — only the pool's LINE locks Tuesday noon, our picks are editable up to each game's real deadline (refined 2026-08-20: min(kickoff, Sunday 16:00 ET) — SNF/MNF lock early at Sunday 4pm, so kickoff-nearest forecasts are later than the real decision time for those games specifically), so kickoff-nearest is still the actual decision-time information set for the bulk of the slate, not `tuesday_noon`**) validates far tighter on the full 2024 season (272 games): temp r=0.972 MAE=3.05°F bias=+0.49°F, wind r=0.718 MAE=3.11mph bias=+2.24mph, confirmed on a 2020-2023 60-game spot check (temp r=0.924 MAE=2.35°F). GFS's own IEM archive reaches back to at least 2005 (measured), so a `kickoff_nearest` archive for 2009-2019 needs no NCEI order — only a `tuesday_noon`-style archive for those seasons remains blocked on the gated HAS/AIRS order. The payoff screen then re-scored the four strongest actual-weather cells above with the Tuesday-noon forecast substituted for the game-time actual, on the 2020-2025 population (see `docs/forecast_weather_screen.md`'s owner-corrected note: this screen used `tuesday_noon` only and was not re-run against the more pool-relevant `kickoff_nearest`): `forecast_weather_warm_team_cold_late` +0.332 pts P+ 0.9711, `forecast_weather_temp_gap_cold_visitor` +0.430 pts P+ 0.9029, `forecast_weather_high_wind_outdoor` -0.059 pts P+ 0.4585, `forecast_weather_dome_team_outdoors_cold` -0.113 pts P+ 0.3165 — all `unresolved_below_power`, recorded in `registry/weak_signals.json`. **2026-08-20: `kickoff_nearest` archive extended to the FULL 2009-2025 window** (`docs/forecast_weather_screen.md` "2026-08-20 extension"; `tuesday_noon` stays blocked pre-2020 -- measured via a live IEM MOS API probe, `model=MEX` returns zero rows for 2009/2015 Tuesday-noon runtimes while `model=GFS` returns 21 rows for the same 2015 runtime, confirmed impossible at the source). 4,431 REG games, 4,379 fetched `ok` (99.98% domestic coverage), instrument validation holds across the full 17-season window (temp r=0.964 MAE=3.25°F; wind r=0.650 MAE=3.15mph) -- no degradation vs. the 2020-2025-only numbers above. A 6-cell family (2 reruns + 4 new: wind x pass-heavy away favorite, precip probability x high total, temp swing since the visitor's last game, dome-team cold+windy) ran on the full window and a 2009-2019-only split, 12 specs, all `unresolved_below_power`. **`warm_team_cold_late` is the strongest read in this project's weather work and the first of these families to clear the crossing-zero bar on both windows**: full +0.1697 pts, 95% [+0.0091, +0.3169], P+ 0.980; pre2020-only +0.2285 pts, [+0.0208, +0.4167], P+ 0.985 (larger pre-2020, era magnitude not absence). `temp_gap_cold_visitor` swung from a coin flip on an early checkpoint to the largest point estimate in the family on final data (+0.27/+0.30 pts, P+ 0.922/0.871) -- three independent constructions of the same mechanism now converge in the 0.87-0.98 P+ range. `wind_passing_away_favorite` inverted on full data (checkpoint P+ 0.996 on n=4 fell to P+ 0.367 on n=22) -- a caution against a 4-game read, not a refutation. `dome_cold_windy` reads weaker than its cold-only parent, a reportable negative lean. Two of six wired same-day as dual-tracked, no-window-cost prospective challengers sharing one live fetch: `forecast_weather_kn_warm_team_cold_late_tilt` and `forecast_weather_kn_precip_high_total_tilt` (`artifacts/prospective/challengers.json`, both `ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE`); the other four left unwired by explicit EV reasoning, notably that `temp_gap_cold_visitor`'s kickoff_nearest form is likely redundant with the already-live `forecast_cold_visitor_tilt` (tuesday_noon cutoff) rather than a reason to add a second challenger for the same mechanism  **2026-09-02 completion:** the playable `pool_decision_2009_2025` archive now covers all 4,431 REG games, with 4,380/4,380 domestic fetches successful and 51 international stadiums explicitly unmappable; validation measured 100% domestic coverage and enforced issuance at or before each game’s real pool deadline. No ATS experiment or active-model change ran. |

None crosses the 0.85/0.15 lead bar; all `unresolved_below_power`. **2026-09-02 implementation follow-through:** `nfl_ats.rest_context` now builds deterministic per-side rest days, rest differential, off-bye, short-week, 9-11-day mini-bye, and true-road streak features from an explicit six-column schedule allowlist. The family is registered but excluded from every model profile; strict input contracts, neutral/season reset behavior, provenance, and a mutation-based leakage regression are pinned in `tests/test_rest_context.py` and documented in `docs/rest_context_features.md`. No experiment was rerun or active model changed. |
| ENV-05 | ✅ | Weather interactions | Wind × passing/kicking style; heat × pace; surface × unit traits. **2026-08-19 evening**: a predeclared 5-cell second-generation battery (`scripts/nfl_weather_followup_screen.py`, `docs/weather_followup.md`) generalized the raw-temperature-threshold cells above into a continuous away-team climatological-gap mechanism and tested wind × pass-heavy visitor directly. Strongest: `weather_followup_temp_gap_cold_visitor` (away team's own-climate outdoor temp minus this game's temp >= 25F) +0.38 full-slate pts, week-blocked [+0.002, +0.754], P+ 0.976, n_flag=240 — narrowly resolved-shaped but from a mined 5-cell family, so recorded `unresolved_below_power` per AGENTS.md, not promoted. `weather_followup_wind_gap_visitor` (wind-sheltered-climate visitor into wind>=15mph) +0.14pts P+ 0.854. `weather_followup_high_wind_pass_heavy_visitor` (prior-season pass rate above median) came back near-null/slightly negative, -0.04pts P+ 0.369 — interval does not sit entirely on the wrong side, so `unresolved_below_power`, not refuted. `weather_followup_rest_disadvantage_cold` +0.02pts P+ 0.606, near-null. All 5 in `registry/weak_signals.json`. **2026-09-11: the forecast-timed, general form of these mechanisms, plus two never-tried cells, ran** (`docs/weather_interactions.md`, `scripts/weather_interactions_screen.py`), on both the deadline-visible `pool_decision` cutoff (playable) and `kickoff_nearest` (secondary), team-long framed so "fade this team" needs no home/away-favorite restriction. `weather_interactions_wind_pass_heavy_fade` (outdoor wind>=15mph × this team's prior-season pass-rate quartile 4, general side not away-favorite-restricted): +5.10pts week-blocked [-1.73,+11.95] P+ 0.928 (pool_decision), +3.97pts P+ 0.874 (kickoff_nearest); on production (opener grade, window [2020,2021], 16 picks changed) +0.44pts [-1.10,+2.16] P+ 0.698. `weather_interactions_wind_fg_reliant_fade` (NEW: wind × prior-season FG-attempts/game quartile 4, attempt-based proxy — no `field_goal_result` field exists in this repo's PBP to build a makes-based version, disclosed): -2.42pts P+ 0.273 (pool_decision), -1.30pts P+ 0.372 (kickoff_nearest), leaning against the predicted fade at both cutoffs and both season halves; on production 0.00pts P+ 0.488 (8 picks changed) — a coin flip at this window's n. `weather_interactions_heat_pace_fade` (NEW: outdoor heat>=85F × this team's prior-season fastest-pace quartile, predeclared FADE): -4.72pts P+ 0.157 (pool_decision), -4.13pts P+ 0.199 (kickoff_nearest) — leans against the predicted direction, and the odd/even split is sharply inconsistent (P+ 0.051 vs 0.589-0.688); on production (5 picks changed) the sign flips to +0.66pts P+ 0.883, a real screen-vs-production divergence reported as measured, not reconciled. `weather_interactions_surface_switch_rush_fade` (NEW: reuses the registered `surface_switch_tilt_overlay` flag × away team's prior-season run-heaviest quartile; no forecast dependency): the strongest screen-level read in the family, +5.67pts P+ 0.959 vs. population (narrows to +2.39pts P+ 0.747 vs. a narrow surface-switch-only control, so most of the raw gap is the already-registered surface-switch main effect), entirely concentrated in odd seasons (P+ 0.995 vs 0.511); on production (17 picks changed) -0.22pts P+ 0.408, again diverging from the screen-level lean. All 4 cells' screen-level and production readings (28 `weak-signals record` + 4 `rotation record` entries, every family drew rotation window [2020, 2021]) are `unresolved_below_power` — no interval sits entirely on the wrong side of zero, no trait has zero split-half reliability (weakest is FG-attempt-rate at 0.087, still nonzero), no positive control was run. **2026-09-11 follow-up: the two cells that leaned the predicted direction on production (cell 1 wind_pass_heavy and cell 3 heat_pace) wired as no-window-cost, dual-tracked prospective challengers** (`forecast_weather_kn_wind_pass_heavy_fade`, `forecast_weather_kn_heat_pace_fade`; `artifacts/prospective/challengers.json`, both `ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE`), mirroring the ENV-01 `forecast_weather_kn_warm_team_cold_late_tilt` / `forecast_weather_kn_precip_high_total_tilt` precedent exactly: each is a post-prediction, parameter-free pick transform (never a retrained model), fades whichever team the model's pick is already on when that team is flagged (home or away, no away-favorite restriction), and shares the SAME live kickoff-nearest GFS-MOS fetch the other two already make every week (`fetch_shared_kickoff_nearest_forecasts_fail_open`, now extended with a `forecast_wind_mph` field parsed from the identical MOS bulletin response's `wsp` field -- zero additional network cost). The pass-rate and pace traits reuse `nfl_ats.experiment_runner`'s existing prior-season quartile/reliability helpers exactly as the screen used them (no rebuild), computed fresh at record time from the newest raw PBP snapshot (wind cell) and the cached `team_season_style.parquet` (heat cell), both wrapped fail-open so a missing/broken trait source degrades to zero flags rather than erroring the publish. Human display names added to `CHALLENGER_DISPLAY_NAMES` ("Wind fades the passing team", "Heat fades the fast team"). **Measured 2026-09-11, confirmed on 2026 Week 1** (active model `d49194e04945a5e5`, 16-game card): both challengers recorded cleanly (14 pre-kickoff decisions each, 2 already past kickoff skipped, no errors) with **0 flips** -- the live MOS fetch returned `fetch_status=ok` for only 1 of 16 games this run (14 `transport_error`, 1 `unmappable_international_stadium`; a direct single-request probe of the same API succeeded, so this reads as a transient/rate-limited run rather than a blocked source) and even that one game did not cross either threshold, so neither tilt had anything to fade this week. Nothing on the served card changed; this is prospective-evidence-only, same as every sibling forecast-weather challenger. |

## Phase 6 — modeling and probability distributions

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-01 | ✅ | Regularized logistic baseline | Time-safe preprocessing and chronological calibration |
| MOD-02 | ✅ | Histogram boosting comparator | Bounded complexity and same evaluation contract |
| MOD-03 | ✅ | Margin regression | Predict conditional mean and compare residual vs market spread |
| MOD-04 | ✅ | Margin distribution | Quantiles or parametric distribution; validate coverage and tails. Coverage re-verified 2026-08-17 on the frozen 2018-2025 artifact: the 50% interval covers 50.21% and the 80% covers 78.94%, both near nominal. **One correctness defect found and fixed in the same pass:** `_three_way_probabilities` tested a CONTINUOUS predictive sample for exact equality with the line, which never fires in floating point, so every published card carried `push_probability = 0.0000` while ~4.8% of integer-line games really push (**9.0% at a line of 3**) and `home_loss_probability` silently absorbed it. The sample is now rounded to integer margins. `home_cover_probability` is deliberately untouched, so no pick moved. The bug survived because the existing test used the `market` target, whose residuals are exact half-integers and made the equality fire by luck; a continuous-residual regression test now pins the realistic path |
| MOD-05 | ✅ | Joint score/total model | Coherent home/away score and total probabilities. **Premise confirmed, ATS payoff undercut (2026-08-17).** The key-number lattice is real, large and stable: P(|margin| = 3) is 14.58% against 5.29% under a fitted normal (2.75x), |margin| = 7 is 8.76% vs 4.83%, ties are 0.29% vs 2.70% (overtime nearly eliminates them), and unimodality is rejected outright (dip 0.0209, p = 0.000 against both uniform and discretised-normal nulls) with the two dominant modes at exactly +3 and -3. A joint score model would reproduce all of it for free. But for *cover* probability it buys ~0.0004 Brier at the settlement line and **zero** over a plain Gaussian at the actual line, because the ATS residual is one line-varying convolution away from smooth (dip p = 1.000; roughness chi2/df 21.2 for raw margin vs 1.6-1.9 for the residual). Build it for push probability, alternative-line/half-point questions and correct-score products -- **not** for ATS accuracy **Correct-score half BUILT and screened (WP23, 2026-09-01; `docs/score_lattice.md`, `nfl_ats.score_lattice`, `artifacts/score_lattice/20260901T192552Z`).** A joint lattice over integer finals conditioned on the market -- the empirical (margin, total) residual cloud recentred on the guess and interpolated onto the score lattice by a mass-preserving triangular kernel of bandwidth 1 (derived: sum_n max(0, 1-|n-x|) = 1), feasible score set enumerated from the data -- walk-forward 2012-2025, 3,829 games, 299 week blocks. **It does NOT replace the shipped exact-score mode list:** top-1 exact score -0.209 accuracy points, week-blocked [-0.495, +0.077], P+ 0.0615 (`score_lattice_top1_exact`, `unresolved_below_power`); any-of-top-3 -1.175, [-1.636, -0.689], P+ 0.0000 -- whole interval below zero, so `score_lattice_top3_exact` is `refuted_mechanism` / `wrong_sign_resolved`. The frozen rule fired NO and `tiebreaker.py` was not changed. **Two products are resolved positive:** the lattice's MEDIAN total beats the shipped weighted-median closest-total answer by +0.263 total points MAE, [+0.162, +0.366], P+ 1.0000 (`score_lattice_closest_total`) -- a free upgrade to the closest-total half of the tiebreak, unwired only because the pool's tiebreak metric is still unrecorded; and P(push), P(margin = m) and alternative-line answers now exist, best built from the UN-recentred lattice (analytically identical to the mode list on exact scores; calibration gap at |line| = 3 of -0.81pp vs the recentred -3.10pp against a realised 8.93% push rate). Week 1 DEN@KC: P(push at KC -2.5) = 0.00% by construction; 6.83% at -3. No card moved. (WP23, 2026-09-01) |
| MOD-06 | ✅ | Bayesian dynamic team model | Partial pooling, uncertainty, explicit offseason evolution. **The coefficient-level arm is closed on measurement (2026-08-17), before being built into the pipeline.** Prototyped on 12,206 free CFB games (rule 8, no window spent): sweeping ridge shrinkage across five orders of magnitude moves forced-pick accuracy under a point, and *increasing* it — the direction the partial-pooling argument predicts — makes the thin-training buckets resolvably **worse** (500–999 rows: .5292 at α=300 → .4646 under evidence-maximised shrinkage). Empirical-Bayes/BayesianRidge/ARD as an accuracy play are dead, as is sample-size-scaled α. ~~**The structural reason, which generalises:** the pool metric reads only `sign(predicted residual)`, and rescaling by any positive scalar cannot change a sign — so *any* scheme whose whole effect is to rescale the prediction is a no-op for the primary goal, however well-motivated. It can move calibration and confidence ordering, never a pick.~~ **RETRACTED 2026-08-17: that reasoning is wrong and was being used to reject work.** The production pick is `home_cover_probability >= 0.5` (`pool.py:41`, `backtest.py:56`) — the median of the out-of-time residual sample shifted by the prediction, not the sign of the prediction. The two rules disagree on **11.8% of the 2,075 scored games** (244), and because that sample's median is non-zero, rescaling the centre *can* flip picks. **And the production rule is resolvably the better of the two: 52.05% vs 49.93%, +2.12 points, season-blocked 95% [+0.24, +4.17], `probability_positive` 0.990** — on the 244 disagreements it wins 59.0%. That entire margin is the residual sample's location offset, currently the unweighted empirical median of a ~500-900-draw trailing holdout that no one has ever modelled. See "Where to look next" in `docs/pool_edge_plan.md`. Independently, ridge penalty changes were never in the "rescale" class at all: generalized ridge gives `b_j = d_j·b_j^OLS/(d_j + λ_j)`, so differing `λ_j` rotate the coefficient vector rather than scaling it (measured: block penalties flip up to 18.6% of CFB picks, a global α change 10→1e4 flips 20.1%, a positive rescale flips exactly 0; `docs/groupwise_ridge.md`). **MOD-06's conclusion still stands on its own measurement above — do not reopen it — but never again reject penalty-structure, calibration, or shrinkage work by citing this corollary.** Type-II ML also optimises squared error, so it correctly shrinks away the small directional signal the forced pick lives on: the Bayesian objective and the pool objective disagree. Two things survive: (a) the warm-up cliff was removed by **evidence rather than a model** — see `docs/rotation_registry.md` rule 9, floor now 2011; (b) the one live arm is **unit-level** shrinkage toward a *position prior* rather than toward zero (`players.py` currently multiplies a thin player's value by `career/(career+200)`, i.e. treats a barely-seen player as worth nothing). That changes *relative* feature values and can therefore flip a pick. Needs no new dependency — closed-form James-Stein in numpy; a full sampler is disqualified anyway because it would inject nondeterminism into a pipeline whose methodology rests on exact reproducibility. Screen on CFB at `probability_positive >= 0.75` before any NFL window. **NFL screen ran 2026-08-19** (`docs/mod06_position_prior_shrinkage.md`, `mod06_position_prior_shrinkage`): the single-variable isolation (production `weak_stack` player-value shrinkage swapped from a zero target to a data-derived position/channel prior, everything else held fixed) scored **-0.048 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [-0.771, +0.672], `probability_positive` 0.4148**, on 2,075 paired games (2018-2025, close grade) — `unresolved_below_power`, no `closing_ground`. `probability_positive` below 0.5 means no EV case today for playing `weak_stack_js_prior` over production; the implementation exists opt-in (`value_shrinkage_target="position_prior"`, `MarginFeatureProfile` `weak_stack_js_prior`) but nothing was wired into `artifacts/active_ats_model.json`. **2026-09-03 rule-comparison re-read on the live profile** (read-only over `artifacts/margins/20260903T143251Z/predictions.parquet`, same 2,075 close-graded games, no window spent): sign rule 51.28% vs production probability rule 52.24% (delta +0.96 pts), 292 disagreements (14.07%) of which production wins 53.42% — same direction as the retired `player`-profile read (244 disagreements, 59.0%, +2.12 pts) at a smaller magnitude. | MOD-08 | ✅ | Distributional boosting | Quantile/NGBoost-style margin and total forecasts. **Completed 2026-09-03:** the smoothing half is promoted and verified end to end — live manifest `probability_method` is `gaussian`, `weekly.py` margin-backtest/margin-predict steps name it explicitly (9 promotion tests pass), and the former ECDF read is tracked prospectively as `ecdf_mapping_incumbent` (`ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE`, 29/35 challengers active) with `smooth_cdf_mapping` retired as `SUPERSEDED_BY_PROMOTION`. The conditioning half stays answered-negative per the 2026-08-17 measurement (no shape signal left to condition on); any new quantile/NGBoost proposal needs its own predeclaration carrying that negative. **Undercut on measurement (2026-08-17); any predeclaration must carry this negative.** MOD-08 targets a richer *conditional* density, but the conditional shape is already near-Gaussian once you condition on the line (residual dip test p = 1.000, roughness chi2/df 1.6-1.9 against 21.2 for the raw margin), and ATS-residual sd is flat at 12.6-13.5 across every spread bucket. A fixed unconditional key-number lattice adds nothing over a plain Gaussian on Brier or log loss (head-to-head probability_positive 0.478). There is no shape signal left to condition on. **What IS worth doing is smoothing, not conditioning:** the production mapping inverts an ECDF built from only ~518 residual draws, which quantises every probability and injects ~2.2pp of Monte-Carlo noise per game; replacing it with any smooth CDF is worth **Brier -0.0015 and log loss -0.0032, week-blocked 95% interval excluding zero (P = 0.998)** on 1,802 non-reserved games -- roughly 9x the effect size the MOD-16 CFB screen could resolve. It also removes an accidental -0.77-point median tilt in the ECDF that currently flips 8.8% of forced picks, so it MOVES PICKS and needs its own predeclared window. **PROMOTED 2026-08-19** (`docs/smooth_cdf_mapping.md`). The Gaussian read was first measured close-graded (weak_stack production recipe, 10 non-reserved seasons, week-blocked accuracy +0.684 pts [-0.444, +1.841], `probability_positive` 0.8666, recorded `mod08_smooth_cdf_mapping`, `unresolved_below_power`), then per AGENTS.md ("grade the decision at the opener; a close-graded number may never veto") graded a SECOND time (multiplicity disclosed) on the predeclared 1,537-paired-game opener archive, production pick rule, week-blocked: accuracy +0.133 pts [-1.397, +1.715], `probability_positive` **0.5536** (recorded `mod08_smooth_cdf_mapping_opener`, also `unresolved_below_power` -- the interval crosses zero, never grounds to reject). The frozen decision rule fires PROMOTE at any `probability_positive` above 0.5 (EV rule for forced picks, not a threshold-clearing rule), so it fired despite the weak margin -- disclosed plainly, not smoothed over: this candidate's edge over the raw ECDF concentrates more at the close (P+ 0.85-0.87) than at the opener (P+ 0.55) on every measurement so far, the opposite pattern from MOD-07's own opener-vs-close story. Brier/log-loss stay resolvably positive at both grades throughout. **What changed:** `nfl_ats.outcomes.score_outcome_week` (the sole production weekly-forecast entry point) now defaults `probability_method="gaussian"`; every other caller (`walk_forward_outcomes`, historical backtests, research scripts) keeps the `"ecdf"` default unchanged. `nfl_ats.active_model`'s SYNCHRONIZED-matching identity now carries `probability_method` (mirroring the existing `calibration_method` field, defaulting to `"ecdf"` for legacy artifacts), so a mismatched-mapping forecast can never silently re-activate the wrong evaluation -- both `weekly.py`'s `margin-backtest`/`margin-predict` steps now pass `--probability-method gaussian` explicitly. Pinned by `tests/test_probability_method_promotion.py` (9 tests). The `smooth_cdf_mapping` prospective challenger is retired (status `SUPERSEDED_BY_PROMOTION`, kept not deleted) and superseded by `ecdf_mapping_incumbent` (`nfl_ats.ecdf_mapping_incumbent_overlay`, 14 tests), which now tracks the FORMER production ECDF read as the challenger against its own successor. Read-only check against the currently published Week 1 card (16 games): exactly **1 pick would flip** under the new default -- `NE at SEA`, from `NE +3.5` (49.89% ECDF) to `SEA -3.5` (51.69% Gaussian); the other 15 picks and the Best Pick nomination (a separate alpha=2000 ranking model, out of scope) are unchanged. Not republished -- CURRENT_PREDICTIONS.md and the live `artifacts/active_ats_model.json` are untouched by this session; republishing is the orchestrator's call **2026-09-07 (read: docs/smooth_cdf_mapping.md superseded note):** Gaussian smoothing stays production; its LOCATION is now the trailing median (MOD-06 promotion, docs/gaussian_median_promotion.md). `ecdf_mapping_incumbent` keeps verifying against the card's recorded method before its ECDF read; the new `gaussian_mean_mapping_incumbent` tracks the former mean read prospectively, so both predecessor arms stay dual-tracked. |

| MOD-12 | ✅ | Hyperparameter protocol | Frozen profile/Ridge/calibration budget selected on prior seasons and scored on next-season folds. **Reopened and then answered on 2026-08-18 (`docs/ridge_alpha.md`): `ridge_alpha = 10.0` is undefended but inert for the metric the pool grades, so it is not worth changing.** Both halves of that are true and neither cancels the other. Measured on the 4,431-game active design: the median principal direction is shrunk **0.274%** (mean 5.50%, weakest decile 15.0%), so the model is unregularised least squares on the signal-bearing bulk. A free 19-point CFB sweep (1e-3 to 1e5, 12,500 games, no window spent) finds **forced-pick accuracy flat across seven orders of magnitude** — nothing beats 10.0 resolvably, and only extreme over-shrinkage at 1e5 is clearly worse. Brier/log-loss IS resolvable and U-shaped, minimised near **α ≈ 2,000-2,500** with the 300-10,000 plateau beating 10.0 at `probability_positive` 0.75-0.97 — but worth only **+0.0003 Brier (~0.12% relative)**, i.e. calibration, not picks. Recommendation: leave the active model alone and route the calibration gain to the Best Pick ranker, which needs no confirmation window. **Two stale figures corrected here:** the widely-quoted "142 columns, rank 71" was the retired `player` profile; the active `weak_stack` design is **90 declared → 159 transformed → rank 82**, on 4,431 games not 4,630. And the null space is **not** mainly the `diff = home − away` identity — **59 of the 77 lost dimensions come from `SimpleImputer(add_indicator=True)`**, because the ≥3-game warm-up rule gates a whole team-state vector atomically, so 45 of 69 indicator columns are bit-for-bit identical (one bit of information, copied 45 times). Exact duplicates change no prediction at any α; they only split coefficient credit. At α=10 the whole group-penalty axis is a no-op (every accuracy delta < 0.09 pts). ~~It deflates the `player_qb_continuity` re-read: α=1 vs α=10 differ by 0.03% vs 0.27% at the median direction, so the ±1.1033-point swing is a calibration of evaluator noise (coin-flip sd on 997 games is ~1.58 points), not a signal~~ — **RETRACTED 2026-08-18: this is the exact reasoning the reclassification refutes.** 1.58 points is an UNPAIRED coin-flip sd; the ±1.1033-point verdict is a PAIRED delta, and the two are not comparable. The 0.03%/0.27% shrinkage argument belongs to a genuinely near-null-contrast SEPARATE alpha-only comparison (`base_alpha1` vs `base_alpha10`, both feature sets held fixed), which flips only 25/997 picks (2.51%) and is resolvably negative on its own. The feature-set contrast that actually carries the ±1.1033 swing has BOTH arms fixed at alpha=1 and differs only in features: it flips **177/997 picks (17.75%, split 94-83)**, paired SE 1.33 pts, MDE80 3.74 pts, `probability_positive` **0.796** (`registry/weak_signals.json`, `player_qb_continuity_matched_alpha`) — a real, if unresolved, lean, not noise **Completion clarification (read 2026-09-02):** `src/nfl_ats/experiments.py` freezes the four-profile × three-alpha × four-calibration candidate budget, builds leak-safe calibrated streams, and selects each held-out season from its prior two seasons; `tests/test_experiments.py` pins the 12 raw fits, 48 declared candidates, outcome consistency guards, and 2020–2025 outer folds. `src/nfl_ats/cli.py` persists candidate, fold, and prediction-level artifacts plus `FROZEN_BEFORE_EVALUATION` metadata. The alpha-10 analysis above is therefore a later parameter-specific finding, not the sole completion evidence for this protocol. |

| MOD-14 | ✅ | Era weighting | Compare rolling training windows and time-decayed sample weights. **Screened, not resolved (2026-08-19, `docs/era_weighting_screen.md`).** A predeclared six-arm grid (exponential season-decay half-lives {2, 4, 8, 16} vs. rolling windows {6, 10} seasons, all vs. a uniform-weight baseline, `ridge_alpha=10.0` unchanged throughout) ran on two independent instruments: the free 12,500-game CFB XLG-03 benchmark (primary screen) and the real production `weak_stack` recipe on 2,047 non-reserved-season NFL close-graded games (secondary, gated on the CFB lean). Both instruments' self-checks reproduce their frozen baselines bit-for-bit before any candidate arm was trusted. **`half_life_8` (8-season exponential decay) is the strongest arm on every one of six cuts across both instruments** — CFB clean-core week-blocked **+0.347 pts [-0.180, +0.863], P+ 0.8987**; NFL week-blocked **+0.684 pts [-0.542, +1.938], P+ 0.8505**, NFL season-blocked **P+ 0.9533** (lower bound -0.096, one hair from excluding zero) — and never resolves negative on any secondary metric. Two disagreements worth carrying forward rather than smoothing over: `half_life_2` is resolved WORSE on NFL Brier/log-loss (week-blocked Brier -0.0044 [-0.0074, -0.0015], P+0.001) despite a positive accuracy lean, and `rolling_6` is resolved WORSE on CFB Brier/log-loss/margin MAE/RMSE (all four P+ <= 0.0033) — accuracy's coarse ~2-point resolution and the continuous metrics disagree, the same pattern `docs/ridge_alpha.md` found. No arm resolves under the binding crossing-zero taxonomy (every accuracy interval contains zero); all twelve arm x league combinations record `unresolved_below_power` in `registry/weak_signals.json` (`era_weighting_cfb_*`, `era_weighting_nfl_*`). This clears the project's 0.75 CFB screen bar for `half_life_8` but not the 0.90 promotion-claim bar, so no production or rotation-registry change was made here — a future session wanting to promote it needs its own predeclared, opener-graded NFL confirmation window **Completion reconciliation 2026-09-02:** the six-arm rolling/decay grid, CFB and NFL screens, opener read, rotation-assigned confirmation, registry recording, and opt-in append-only `era_weighted_half_life_8` challenger are all implemented. The opener confirmation remains `unresolved_below_power` at -0.2193 accuracy points, 95% [-3.3898,+2.6786], `probability_positive` 0.4246; completing the engineering/research row does not reclassify that result or promote it. Twenty focused challenger contracts passed on 2026-09-02. |
| MOD-15 | ✅ | Temporal schedule-graph ratings | Leak-safe PageRank/HITS and ridge/SRS comparator completed; graph selected in 0/8 outer seasons and was not promoted (underlying artifact not retained — prose-only record, see `docs/closure_audit.md` §3) |
| MOD-16 | ✅ | Conditional margin variance | Replace the pooled residual distribution with game-level heteroskedasticity; accepted only if held-out cover/push/loss calibration beats the pooled baseline. The predeclared CFB screen (August 2026, `docs/margin_variance.md`) **failed**: a Ridge scale model on mismatch/total/pace/experience made clean-core cover log-loss resolvably worse (week-blocked [−0.00056, −0.00012]) — the pooled distribution is already near-correctly calibrated. Only a genuinely NFL-specific variant (QB/backup status, weather) with a new ledger-aware predeclaration remains admissible, and it is deprioritized by this result |

| OPS-05 | ✅ | Tuesday lock moved to the pool's noon spread lock | Owner, 2026-09-08, quoting the pool's own notice that spreads lock Tuesday at 12:00 PM, and asking why the Week 1 lock was still scheduled for 09:15 after that had been stated. The card had been formed at a 09:00 capture and graded against a line that could still move for three hours. **Fixed the same morning, before the old 09:00 job fired (daemon restarted 08:29 ET on the new schedule):** `odds_tue_open` now runs at 12:05 ET (the first odds capture of the day, landing just after the pool lock, so it IS the pool's line), `odds_tue_open_halves` with it, `weekly_lock` at 12:20 (closes 14:20), Tuesday's daily lineup refresh at 14:30 so two weekly-runs never overlap; the separate noon job was dropped as redundant; the one-click refresh refuses a Tuesday spread capture before 12:05 (the opener is the earliest Tuesday quote per book, so an earlier capture would become the opener). Picks are due at each game's own kickoff, not at noon, so nothing is lost. `scripts/tuesday_line_gap.py` stays for any week that gets an earlier capture. Open question kept honest: the historical `tue_open` archive is the 09:00 line, so the archive grade is three hours earlier than the pool's line; the 09:00-to-noon move is not measured on those seasons. | 2026-09-08: schedule changed, tests pinned, daemon restarted. Same day, after the audit found a legacy Task Scheduler entry firing a 09:00 capture: lane AB (Fable) made the live opener rule itself robust -- `POOL_SPREAD_LOCK_ET` 12:00 ET, per book the earliest Tuesday quote at or after the lock, median over post-lock books, labelled `opener_basis` (post_lock / pre_lock_fallback); the historical `tue_open` archive pinned bit-for-bit; DST-tested; the 09:00 snapshot restored under the new rule (it reads `pre_lock_fallback` until the 12:05 capture lands). Lane AB also established that the card's decision line is the nflverse schedule spread at lock time, not the Odds API capture -- the opener capture grades the archive and feeds the live-opener consumers (Best Pick nomination, CLV), so the noon move matters through the 12:20 ingest as much as the capture. First noon lock 2026-09-08 12:05/12:20 ET. |

Topic in `NFL_ATS_NTFY_TOPIC` (user environment, random name, never in the repo); test message delivered 2026-09-10 18:40 ET; daemon restarted with it before the 19:15 Thursday refresh. `docs/pick_change_notifications.md`. |

| LEAD-08 | ✅ | Friday designation momentum | DNP→LP (healing trajectory) vs LP→DNP (deteriorating) vs static Friday labels predict Sunday status beyond the label itself. Predeclared direction: FADE teams with net-negative trajectories. Instrument: NFL.com 2022–24 Wed/Thu/Fri revision depth; distinct from the static OUT-count screen (trajectory, not level). **2026-09-05 CX14 measured: recorded unresolved refresh screen on 1,047 production-paired opener games; 0 flips, +0.000 accuracy points, probability_positive=0.00000, probability_tie=1; zero construct coverage, no directional mechanism evidence; inactive oracle unavailable; docs/injury_trajectory_leads.md.** **2026-09-11 measured (LEAD-08 lane):** confirmed a third way that the literal Wed/Thu/Fri per-player trajectory has zero coverage in every ingested source — the canonical `data/raw/nflverse_injuries/20260910T203021Z/injuries.parquet` (76,784-class PER-03 history) stores exactly one row per player-week (90,887/90,889 season/week/team/gsis_id groups have count 1), so no player ever has both an early-week and a Friday observation to sequence; this is a data-availability fact, not a mechanism finding, and not a closing ground. Built the closest available proxy instead — a revision-timing class (`static`: date_modified's Eastern weekday never moves past Wed; `revised_late`: it does) — and ran the full pipeline on it. Base rates (76,554 covered player-weeks, 2010–2024): `revised_late` inactive (snap-count-proxy) rate 46.886% (n=70,123) vs `static` 45.685% (n=6,431), same-direction gap at every practice level (DNP +14.30pp, LP +5.09pp, FP +2.52pp). Team-game feature = trailing snap share (role_share reused from `_injury_features`) × season-lagged walk-forward P(inactive\|class), stacked on PRODUCTION (`pick_home_at_open_probability_rule`, active model `d49194e04945a5e5`) via a discrete asymmetric-flag fade rule. Rotation family `friday_designation_momentum_on_production` assigned window **[2020, 2021]** (matches LEAD-62's window): **20 picks changed** of 456 games, production 53.2895% vs candidate 52.8509%, **−0.4386 accuracy points, 95% [−1.7660, +0.8889], probability_positive 0.2679**; full 2020–2025 population: **50 picks changed** of 1,503 games, effect **0.0000, 95% [−0.7984, +0.8005], probability_positive 0.502975** (2025 contributes zero coverage — date_modified is null for every 2025 row). Positive control (realized margin) on the assigned window: +46.7105 points, P+ 1.0 — harness proven sensitive. Sign is not uniform across seasons (2020 +0.45 P+0.71, 2021 −1.27 P+0.12, 2022 +1.61 P+0.82), so `wrong_sign_resolved` is inadmissible; reliability is positive, so `no_split_half_reliability` is inadmissible. Recorded `unresolved_below_power` via both `nfl-ats rotation record` and two `nfl-ats weak-signals record` calls (window + full-population); registry now 5,586 NFL weak-signal rows. THE DIRECTION WAS CHOSEN AFTER SEEING THIS ROW'S SIGN -- stated plainly in the challenger registration and the doc as a post-hoc reversal, graded ONLY on 2026+ games, never as a retroactive claim on this row's spent 2020-2021 window. | LEAD-18 | ✅ | Concussion recovery by position | Skill-position Wednesday-concussion DNPs sit more often than line-concussion DNPs (recovery curves differ). Concussion+DNP player-weeks sit out Sunday 99.13% of the time REGARDLESS of position (1,254 of 1,265) — a ceiling effect leaving little room for a positional split. Skill-minus-line sit-rate gap, season-blocked bootstrap (2,000 draws, seed 20260905): 2013-2024 **-0.00328, 95% [-0.01674, +0.00849], P+ 0.3365** (n=452 skill/359 line); by era 2013-2017 **+0.00701, P+ 0.7765**; 2018-2024 **-0.01145, 95% [-0.02536, 0.00000], P+ 0.0000** (line rate is a literal 1.0, 196/196, mechanically flooring the upper tail — CI touches but does not clear zero, so `wrong_sign_resolved` does not apply per AGENTS.md). Predeclared direction not supported by this population; comparison population (non-concussion DNP) DOES show a robust, whole-interval-positive skill-vs-line gap both eras (2013-2024: **+0.09612, 95% [+0.07513,+0.11674], P+ 1.0**), so the generic position-group split stays justified, it just does not extend to concussions. No `nfl_ats.weak_signals.EFFECT_UNITS` value is commensurable with a raw sit-rate gap (rate difference, not an ATS/forecast metric); per AGENTS.md's commensurability rule, NOT recorded in the weak-signals registry — this row, `docs/injury_report_hygiene.md`, and `artifacts/injury_report_hygiene/20260905T044503Z/results.json` are the record. |
| LEAD-19 | ✅ | Personal-matter DNP exclusion audit | "Personal reasons" DNPs resolve unpredictably; measure their Sunday-action base rate to justify EXCLUDING them from availability features. No ATS direction (feature-hygiene measurement, valuable either way). **2026-09-05 measured, decision: keep-with-separate-flag** (`docs/injury_report_hygiene.md`, same code as LEAD-18; population 2013-2024, see that row's note on the disclosed 2025 `date_modified` gap): frozen exact-match designation vocabulary (5 disjoint buckets, full list in the doc) applied to 17,508 DNP player-weeks. Personal-matter DNPs play the following Sunday **53.3%** of the time (n=227, all 2018-2024 — zero personal-matter rows exist 2013-2017), far above genuine-injury DNPs at the same practice status (**7.9%**, n=14,752; gap **+0.454, 95% [+0.265,+0.605], P+ 1.0**) and far below rest-day DNPs (**95.7%**, n=897; gap **-0.423, 95% [-0.635,-0.253], P+ 0.0**) — personal-matter is statistically distinct from both categories a feature designer might fold it into. Decision implied: neither exclude (53.3% is informative) nor merge into either neighboring bucket (would bias both away from their true rates); the specific mixed cell this risk is concrete for (`report_status` blank + DNP practice status, the combo `nfl_ats.availability`'s learned-rate table already keys on) mixes played rates 30.3%-96.7% across designations within one pooled cell today (766 genuine-injury rows are only 28.7% of that 2,672-row cell). Same commensurability conclusion as LEAD-18: no `EFFECT_UNITS` value fits a rate difference, NOT recorded in the weak-signals registry; doc + `artifacts/injury_report_hygiene/20260905T044503Z/results.json` are the record. Valuable either way, per this row's own predeclaration: the audit answers the exclude/keep/flag question regardless of which way the numbers came out. |

Closest-total tie-break simulation (public field declared before scoring: guess ~ market total + Normal(+1.5, sd 6), 20,000-draw Monte Carlo, common random numbers, season-blocked bootstrap) at the already-served **-1 shade**: mean-absolute-error improvement **+0.0476**, 95% [-0.0509, +0.1502], **P+ 0.816**; win-rate improvement vs the served guess -0.137pp, 95% [-0.935, +0.661], P+ 0.362 (crosses zero -- the win-rate metric is far less sensitive to a 1-point shade against an already-biased public field than the AE metric is). Widening the shade past -1 does not help: -2 AE P+ 0.610, -3 AE P+ 0.376 (crosses zero despite a negative point estimate), and **-4 is RESOLVED negative on win-rate** (whole 95% interval [-6.782, -0.813], P+ 0.005; recorded `refuted_mechanism` / `wrong_sign_resolved` -- this closes the -4 arm only, not -1/-2/-3). The already-served model-lean blend (`TOTALS_RESIDUAL_WEIGHT`=0.1 x `predicted_residual`, 261 model-covered games 2010-2025) does not add edge beyond a fixed shade: AE P+ 0.190, win-rate P+ 0.215. A secondary closest-EXACT-SCORE reading (Manhattan distance; POL-12 never recorded which rule the pool actually uses) leans the OTHER way for the -1 shade (AE P+ 0.257, win-rate P+ 0.121) -- the AE benefit is specific to the closest-total reading. Chronological check: the AE metric alone, trained only on 2009-2017, already selects -1 as best (matching the predeclared direction); scored on the untouched 2018-2025 held-out seasons, that same -1 shade holds **AE P+ 0.843** (win-rate P+ 0.428, flat, consistent with the full-sample read) -- the AE edge replicates out-of-sample rather than evaporating. 14 results recorded via `nfl-ats weak-signals record`, family `tiebreaker_low_side_shading`, category `market` (registry `registry/weak_signals.json`). Decision: nothing here changes what is already served (the -1 shade in `docs/prospective_bestpick_tiebreaker.md`/`tiebreaker.json`, untouched by this lane) -- the predeclared -1 direction is EV-favoured on the closest-total reading and replicates out-of-sample; only a much larger, un-served -4 shade is refuted. The pool's real tie-break rule (closest total vs. closest score) remains uncaptured; the prospective weekly arm this row already tracks continues unaffected. |
